Let's design a full search pipeline:

Function propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400):
- Determine deadline.
- Generate initial candidates of several lengths (n in [256,512,1024,2048 maybe length of height_sequence_1 if moderate]). For each, generate variants:
    1. uniform (maybe not best but baseline).
    2. exponential skew λ in list [-2,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,2] (f=exp(λ i/(n-1))). For negative, shift/reverse.
    3. "weighted exponential with cosine": f=exp(λ x)*(1 + a*cos(2π*x + φ) + b*sin(2π*x+ψ)) clipped nonnegative, positive floor. Optimize a,b,φ,ψ by random? Too many.
    4. Gaussian mixture: 1-3 bumps with means, widths, weights chosen random; also deterministic: one bump skewed? Evaluate.
    5. Provided parent normalized (if finite). If length in [128,8192], include and maybe downsample to n? Could use as length 3809? 3809 conv O(14M) okay but LP maybe large. We can include one evaluation of parent.
- Score using fast_score (FFT) not exact to avoid O(n^2) early. Then exact evaluate for top candidates.
- Local optimize top candidates using one or more methods:
    A) Exponentiated-gradient / Adam smooth max on full p.
    B) LP best-response if n <= 1024 maybe 20 iterations.
    C) Block perturb local search using fast score: random block scaling/masking, hill climb. This can polish.

Need ensure time. Let's detail algorithms.

Fast objective:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    L = 1
    while L < 2*a.size-1: L <<=1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*len(a)-1]
    return c
```
For real nonnegative, product no conjugate. But due to FFT, small negative values possible; max will be ~positive. For score, b=max(c), if b<0 due error set 0. Normalize sum? For internal maintain sum=1. Score = 2*n*max_b (since sum=1). If using nonnormalized, 2n max/S^2.
```
def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    a=a/s
    c=fast_conv(a)
    return 2*len(a)*max(c[0], np.max(c))
```
But if we maintain sum=1, score=2n max_b.

Clipping? For internal nonnegative. For output, scale to max ≤1000: if max>0 scale =1000/max(p) else 1. Sum may be >0.01. Output scaled. But if p has zeros and max small? Scale invariance unchanged unless clipping. Ensure max p after scale 1000. If p min? okay. If p all zero invalid; we maintain positive floor.

Need exact evaluate_sequence for actual score. In propose, call evaluate_sequence(candidate_scaled.tolist()) for best few times. But if p sum=1 scale to 1000, values up to1000, sum=1000*sum p/max =1000/max. If max p extremely small (<0.01/1000?) Could sum <0.01? If max p <1e-5 after scaling, sum <0.01 invalid. But for sequences of moderate n, max p not too small if normalized sum=1: max p ≥1/n. For n up to 100000, 1/n=1e-5, scaled sum=100. >0.01. For n up to 1e7, maybe; we won't. Good.

Exponentiated gradient optimizer:
```
def exp_grad_optimize(p, n_iters, eta_schedule, beta_scale, time_limit, verbose=False):
    p=p.copy()
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    theta? Not needed if multiplicative.
    for it in range(n_iters):
       b=fast_conv(p)
       m=b.max()
       if not finite break
       # weights
       beta = beta_scale / max(m, 1e-12)  # m~1/n
       u = np.exp(beta*(b-m))
       u /= u.sum()
       # gradient
       grad = crosscorr(p, u)
       # Project to tangent: subtract mean
       grad = grad - grad.mean()
       # multiplicative step with adaptive eta maybe based on norm
       eta = base_eta / (n? ) maybe
       p = p * np.exp(eta * (-grad)) # minimize: p_i exp(-eta grad_i)
       # Normalize (also ensures sum)
       if p.sum()<=0 or not finite: break
       p /= p.sum()
       # optional floor to avoid underflow
       p = np.clip(p, 1e-12, None); p /= p.sum()
       score=fast_score(p)
       if score<best: update; else if no improvement for many, reduce eta/ temp or restart.
```
But note gradient of smoothed max wrt p_i is Σ_k u_k ∂b_k/∂p_i = crosscorr(p,u). Since b_k = Σ_l p_l p_{k-l}; derivative wrt p_i: p_{k-i} for valid (ordered derivative? If b_k quadratic, derivative is 2? Let's check: b_k = Σ_j p_j p_{k-j}. Derivative wrt p_i: terms j=i => p_{k-i}; terms k-j=i => j=k-i => p_i? Actually derivative of p_j p_{k-j}: if j=i gives p_{k-i}; if k-j=i gives p_{k-i} as well but same term? When j runs, each unordered pair may appear twice except diagonal. The derivative from full sum: ∂/∂p_i Σ_j p_j p_{k-j} = p_{k-i} (from j=i) + p_{k-i} (from j=k-i) if i != k-i? LP best-response optimizer:
```
def lp_perturb(p, max_iters=20, t=0.5, time_limit):
    for it:
       b=fast_conv(p); M=max(b)
       A = build_conv_matrix(p*n) sparse
       res=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
       if success:
           h=res.x
           s=h.sum()
           if s>0:
              q=h/s
              newp=(1-t)*p + t*q
              newp=np.clip(newp,0,None); normalize
              score=fast_score(newp)
              if score<score_p:
                  p=newp
                  # maybe update t smaller
              else:
                  t*=0.7
    return p,best
```

def project_simplex(v):
    # minimize ||x-v|| s.t. x>=0, sum x=1
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)-1
    cond=u-csvv/u[:len(u)]>0? Need standard.
    n_ = u>0
    # simpler use `scipy.optimize`? Implement:
    def proj_simplex(v):
       n=v.size
       u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
       cssv=np.cumsum(u)-1.0
       cond=u - cssv/np.arange(1,n+1) > 0
       if not np.any(cond): return np.ones(n)/n
       rho=np.argmax(cond)  # but cond true for initial; need last true
       theta=cssv[rho]/(rho+1)
       return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```

def build_toeplitz_conv(p):
   n=len(p)
   # A rows 0..2n-2, cols 0..n-1, A[k,j] = p[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n
   # Use p scaled n maybe

   row_indices=[] col_indices=[] data=[]

       data.extend(P)
       col_indices.extend([j]*n)
       row_indices.extend(range(j, j+n))
   A = sparse.csr_array((data,(row_indices,col_indices)), shape=(2n-1,n))
```

3. `block_local_search(p0, time_limit)`:
   - Use fast score. Iterate random block multiplicative. Blocking can improve after gradient. Use `rng`.
   ```
   best=p; best_score=fast_score(p)
   sigma=0.5
   for it while time:
      n=len(p)
      width = int(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(max(2,n//1000)), np.log(max(width, n//8)))))
      start = rng.integers(0,n-width)
      cand = p.copy()
      # choose operation:
      if rng.random()<0.7: cand[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,size=width))
      else if <0.9: cand[start:stop] = 0 (or small) and compensate later
      else: cand[start:stop] += rng.normal(0,sigma*p[start:stop])? 
      normalize, floor.
      if score<best: accept, sigma=min(sigma*1.05,2) else sigma*=0.95; no_improve...
   ```

Optimization loop:
```
def optimized_grad(p0, max_time, name):
    p=normalize(p0)
    best=p; best_score=score_fast(p)
    beta_range=10.0  # exponent range
    step_target=1.0
    no_improve=0
    last_time=time
    it=0
    while time-left > 1:
       b=fft_conv(p)
       m=b.max()
       if not finite: break
       beta=beta_range/m if m>0 else 0
       weights=np.exp(beta*(b-m)); weights/=weights.sum()
       grad = 2.0 * crosscorr_ones(p, weights)
       grad = grad - grad.mean()
       # Optional: random subgradient if multiple near max
       # multiplicative:
       g = grad - np.mean(grad)
       scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
       if scale <= 0: break
       eta = step_target / scale
       # Limit factors
       factors = np.exp(np.clip(-eta*g, -4, 4))
       p_new = p * factors
       p_new = np.clip(p_new, 1e-12, None)
       p_new /= p_new.sum()
       score = score_fast(p_new)
       it++
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best = p_new; best_score=score
           no_improve=0
           if random? step_target*1.05
       else:
           p = p? We can still set p=p_new to explore? If score worse, maybe revert? For local minima, accept occasionally (simulated annealing)? Use occasional non-improving moves to escape.
```
Should we accept non-improving? For nonconvex, yes. But keep best. We can do simulated annealing: accept if score < best or with probability exp(-(score-best)/T) T decreasing. But score objective not huge; use relative. This helps escape.
However multiplicative gradient descent with revert may oscillate. Use adaptive: maintain current p; if new not improve, we can still move if mild (within 1%); else revert and reduce step. Simpler: accept if score < current_score + tolerance, revert otherwise. current_score tracked. Use temperature small.

Step target schedule: start 0.5, if no improve reduce to 0.1; if improve increase. Randomize.

Potential issue: Multiplicative update `p*exp(-eta g)` can make zeros? floor. But if p_i small and g positive large, factor e^-4; okay. If g negative, factor up to e^4, may blow up but normalized; okay.

Crosscorr implementation via FFT:
```
def crosscorr_p_weights(p, u):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    N=1
    while N < n + L -1: N <<=1  # 3n-2
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p_rev,N)
    Fu=np.fft.rfft(u,N)
    g=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fu,N)[n-1:n-1+n]
    return g
```
Check alignment: We derived g_i = (p_rev * u)_{n-1+i}. Good. u length L; zero-padded. For i=0, conv index n-1 sum r p_rev[r] u[n-1-r]? r? p_rev[r]=p[n-1-r], l=n-1-r => u[i+l]=u[n-1-r]? Not matching? Let's test with small manually.
We want g_i = Σ_{l=0}^{n-1} p_l u_{i+l}.
Take p_rev[r]=p_{n-1-r}. Conv c_k=Σ_r p_rev[r] u[k-r]. Set k=n-1+i.
Then c=Σ_r p_{n-1-r} u[n-1+i-r]. Let l=n-1-r => r=n-1-l, l=0..n-1. u[n-1+i-(n-1-l)] = u[i+l]. Good. Sum_l p_l u[i+l]. Perfect.

But weights length L=2n-1, u indices up to 2n-2. For i=n-1, l=n-1 => index 2n-2 valid. Good.

Can compute also using `np.fft.irfft(np.conj(Fp)*Fu)?` Our derivation uses convolution (not correlation) with p_rev. Since p_rev and u real, Fp*Fu works. Good.

Gradient factor 2? b convolution p*p. ∂b_k/∂p_i = p_{k-i} + p_{k-i}? Let's verify with c_k = Σ_j p_j p_{k-j}. For a fixed i, terms where j=i and where k-j=i. If i != k-i, two distinct terms in sum each p_i p_{k-i}; so derivative 2 p_{k-i}. If i=k-i (k=2i), term j=i appears once p_i^2 derivative 2p_i. So derivative = 2 p_{k-i} (with out-of-range p=0). Good. Our grad =2 crosscorr. For uniform, u symmetric, crosscorr constant? yes.

If using smooth max L = (m + logsumexp(beta(b-m))/beta). Its gradient is 2 crosscorr with weights normalized. Good.

Can we compute gradient faster using direct for small active? FFT okay.

Update with gradient: In mirror descent, we should use `grad_i / p_i`? The multiplicative update p_i exp(-η ∂F_i) is standard for entropy projection. The natural gradient for Euclidean step in log coordinates is ∂F_i/p_i. But using ∂F_i in exponent is also common (exponentiated gradient for Dinkelbach) but step size scales with p? Let's derive: We want solve min F(p) over simplex with Frank-Wolfe? Actually exponentiated gradient for minimizing F with Bregman divergence D_KL(p_new||p) uses update p_i(λ) = p_i exp(-η F'(p_i)/λ) (for linear F). For nonlinear, same if F convex; natural. It uses gradient in probability coordinates, not divided by p_i. If F' ~ constant for all p_i, p_i factors cancel. If grad_i ~1/n, p_i~1/n, exponent η grad ~η/n; to get O(1) changes need η~n. Step_target/scale with scale~1/n gives η~n*target. Good. So okay.

But if p_i very small and grad_i similar, small p changes large relative? That's entropy scaling; may keep diversity.

Maybe use `log_grad = grad / p` and update p*exp(-η log_grad); scale of log_grad O(1), eta ~ target. This is natural gradient in log space. Then p_i factors don't cancel. Which is better? The mirror descent with KL divergence uses grad_i (not divided) because Bregman D_KL(x||p) derivative -log(x/p)+...; optimality x∝ p exp(-η grad_i). Yes grad_i. If using log-softmax Euclidean gradient, use grad_i/p_i. Both equivalent up to scaling? p_i p? Not same.

th = np.log(p + eps) # not normalized? Actually p=softmax(th). Starting th=log p but p sum1 not necessarily exp sum; softmax(th) = p/sum exp log p = p. Good.

- Keep `cur_score`.
- `T` starts e.g. (cur_score-best_score?) but score near 1.5 differences 0.01. Set absolute T=0.05 decaying to 0. Accept if new_score <= cur_score + T*rand? Objective scale ~1; use 0.01. Let's design `block_perturb_search(p, max_time, rng)`:
```
cur=p.copy(); cur_score=score_fast(p)
best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
sigma=0.3
last=time
while time-left>5:
  n=len(cur)
  # width choose log uniform [max(1,n//1000), n//5]
  lo=max(2,int(n//1000)); hi=max(lo+1,int(n//5))
  width=int(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(lo), np.log(hi))))
  start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
  cand=cur.copy()
  op=rng.random()
  if op<0.55:
    cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,size=width))
  elif op<0.75:
    # random noise additive scaled
    noise = rng.normal(0, sigma*cur[start:...].max() if max>0 else sigma/n, size=width)
    cand[start:...]=np.maximum(cand[start:...]+noise,0)
  elif op<0.9:
    # zero a subblock
    sub=width//4
    off=integers(0,width-sub+1) if sub>0 else 0
    cand[start+off:start+off+sub] = 0.0
  else:
    # swap or copy block from elsewhere
    if n>2*width:
       src=integers(0,n-width+1)
       cand[start:...]=cur[src:src+width]
  normalize floor
  score=score_fast(cand)
  if score < best_score - 1e-12:
     best=cand; best_score=score
     cur=cand
     sigma=min(2.0, sigma*1.02)
  else:
     if score < cur_score + 0.001*max(1,cur_score) and rng.random()<0.2: # accept mild
        cur=cand; cur_score=score; sigma=max(0.05, sigma*0.98)
     else:
        sigma=max(0.02, sigma*0.98)
  # early stop if no improve many and sigma small? continue until time
```
This can improve.

Need `fft_conv` stable for many calls. Let's implement with `np.fft.rfft` and precompute transform length? For fixed n per optimizer, we can create a `Convolver` object? But helper top-level no closure? We can have top-level class? Rules: helper functions top-level no closures from function nesting. Classes okay? Probably, but simpler functions with cache? We can cache FFT lengths? Not necessary.
```
def fft_conv_same(a):
    n = a.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = 1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    ca = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return ca[:L]
```
For gradient crosscorr, length M = n + (2n-1)-1 = 3n-2, N pow2. Could compute from same rfft lengths. Since u length 2n-1. We can compute each iteration; okay.

Potential issue: `f * f` for FFT computes linear convolution? Yes if zero-padded to at least L. `fa` of a length N; product inverse gives circular conv, zero-padding ensures linear. Good.

For negative small values in conv, max could be slightly negative if all zero? p positive, max positive. For gradient, weights based on b; small negative at edges could get lower weight; fine. But if due round a central coefficient computed lower than true but another small positive? irrelevant.

Now, initial profile generation more concrete.

```
def make_profiles(n, rng):
    xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)  # include both ends? For discrete, maybe endpoint=False; include end may duplicate boundary. Use np.linspace(0,1,n) okay.
    profs=[]
    # uniform
    profs.append(np.ones(n))
    # exponential
    for lam in [-2,-1.5,-1,-0.75,-0.5,-0.25,0.25,0.5,0.75,1,1.5,2]:
       val=np.exp(lam*(xs-0.5))
       profs.append(np.abs(val)+1e-6)
    # power
    for a in [-1.5,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,1.5,2]:
       val=(xs+1e-3)**a
       profs.append(np.abs(val))
       val2=((1-xs)+1e-3)**a
       profs.append(val2)
    # sinusoids
    for d in [0, np.pi/4, np.pi/2, 3*np.pi/4, np.pi, 5*np.pi/4, 3*np.pi/2, 7*np.pi/4]:
       val=1+0.8*np.cos(2*np.pi*xs+d)
       # also amplitude 1 with phase? floor
       profs.append(np.maximum(val,1e-3))
       val=1+0.8*np.sin(2*np.pi*xs+d)
       profs.append(np.maximum(val,1e-3))
    # product exp * sinusoid
    for lam in [-1,0,1]:
       base=np.exp(lam*(xs-0.5))
       for d in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi, 3*np.pi/2]:
          for amp in [0.4,0.8,1.0]:
             val=base*(1+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*xs+d))
             profs.append(np.maximum(val,1e-4))
    # gaussian mixtures
      means choices: [0.5], [0.3,0.7], [0.2,0.8], [0.1,0.9], [0.3,0.6,0.9], [0.2,0.5,0.8]
      widths [0.1,0.2,0.3,0.45]
      weights [0.3,0.7], [0.1,0.9], [0.5,0.5]
      construct val=sum w*exp(-0.5*((xs-c)/w)^2)
    # random smooth: use rng around
      for _ in range(50):
         D=5; x=xs
         amp_sum=0; val=1.0
         for m in range(1,D+1):
             amp = rng.random()*0.2; if amp_sum+amp>0.9: amp=0.9-amp_sum
             amp_sum+=amp
             phase=rng.uniform(0,2π)
             val += amp*np.cos(2π*m*xs+phase)
         profs.append(np.maximum(val,1e-3))
```

def next_pow2(m):
    return 1 << (int(max(1,m)) -1).bit_length()
```

Fast conv:
```
def fft_conv_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
```
Could use `np.fft.rfft` each call. For fixed n, overhead. Fine.

Crosscorr:
```
def fft_crosscorr_p_weights(p, u):
    n=p.size
    L=u.size
    if L < 2*n-1:
       # pad
       upp=np.zeros(2*n-1); upp[:L]=u; u=upp
    N=1 << (n + u.size - 1 -1).bit_length()
    pr=p[::-1]
    return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(pr,N)*np.fft.rfft(u,N), N)[n-1:n-1+n]
```

Score fast:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0: return np.inf
    a=a/s
    b=fft_conv_same(a)
    if b.size==0: return np.inf
    m=float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    if m < 0: m=0.0
    return 2.0*a.size*m
```
Since sum1. If m slightly >? fine.

Normalize:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=1e-12):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not finite: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    a = np.maximum(a, floor*s)?? If floor absolute 1e-12, sum changes.
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    a /= a.sum()
    return a
```
Using absolute floor may bias if n large (n*1e-12 sum negligible). Good. But if a has many zeros and max small, floor may increase sum; okay.

    v=np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    v=np.maximum(v,0.0)
    if size is not None and v.size != size: ... # skip
    s=v.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return None
    return v/s
```

```
def resample_profile(p, new_n):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    n=p.size
    if n == new_n: return p.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n)
    q=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    return normalize_profile(q)
```
Linear interp of density; if p has sharp, okay.

Now, gradient optimizer top-level:
```
def exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p0, max_time, rng, name="eg"):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p0)
    n=p.size
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fast(p)
    cur_score=best_score
    step_target=1.0
    beta_range=10.0
    no_improve=0
    last=time.monotonic()
    deadline=last+max_time
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()+0.1 < deadline:
        b = fft_conv_same(p)
        m = float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0: break
        beta=beta_range/m
        wb = np.exp(beta*(b-m))
        sw = wb.sum()
        if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <=0:
            # fallback active top
            k=int(min(b.size, 32))
            idx=np.argsort(b)[-k:]
            wb=np.zeros(b.size); wb[idx]=1.0; sw=k
        u=wb/sw
        grad = 2.0 * fft_crosscorr_p_weights(p, u)
        grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-16:
            # do a random block perturb manually? maybe break
            # For this optimizer, if no gradient, stop
            break
        eta = step_target / scale
        # to avoid huge factors, clip exponent
        logfac = -eta*grad
        logfac = np.clip(logfac, -4.0, 4.0)
        p_new = p * np.exp(logfac)
        p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 1e-12)
        p_new /= p_new.sum()
        score = score_fast(p_new)
        it += 1
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = p_new.copy(); best_score=score
            cur_score = score
            no_improve=0
            # increase step a bit
            step_target = min(3.0, step_target*1.1)
        else:
            # accept if mild improvement over current or random
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < cur_score + 0.002*max(1.0,cur_score):
                p = p_new.copy(); cur_score=score
                step_target = max(0.2, step_target*0.97)
                no_improve += 1
            else:
                # revert; reduce step; occasionally reset to best?
                p = best.copy() if no_improve > 50 else p
                cur_score = score_fast(p) if p is best? expensive? We can keep best_score
                step_target = max(0.2, step_target*0.85)
                no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 200:
                # random restart from best with perturbation
                p = apply_block_perturb(best, rng, single=True)
                cur_score = score_fast(p)
                no_improve=0
        if it % 200 == 0:
             print(f"[{name}] it={it} score={best_score:.6f}")
    return best, best_score
```
Need avoid nested apply_block_perturb; define top-level `random_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.5)`.

But if `p=best.copy()` and `cur_score` not updated? Use `cur_score=best_score`. Good.

However, `score_fast(p_new)` recomputes conv, but we already computed b for p not p_new. Need score each candidate. That's one FFT extra (for gradient we computed p conv; score p_new another). Could optimize by computing score for p_new using same? no. Accept.

Maybe use analytic gradient with top active set to reduce non-differentiability? This is fine.

`apply_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.5)`:
```
def random_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.5):
    p=p.copy(); n=p.size
    if n < 4: return p
    lo=max(2,n//1000); hi=max(lo+2,min(n, max(4,n//4)))
    width=int(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(lo), np.log(hi))))
    start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
    # choose mode
    r=rng.random()
    if r<0.6:
       p[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width))
    elif r<0.8:
       p[start:start+width] = 0.0
    else:
       src=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
       p[start:start+width] = p[src:src+width]
    p=np.clip(p,1e-12,None)
    p/=p.sum()
    return p
```

Block local search function:
```
def block_local_search(p0, max_time, rng):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p0); cur=score_fast(p); best=p.copy(); best_score=cur; sigma=0.3; deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time; no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       cand=random_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma)
       score=score_fast(cand)
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=cand; best_score=score; p=cand; cur=score; no_improve=0; sigma=min(2.0, sigma*1.03)
       else:
           if score < cur + 0.001*max(1,cur) and rng.random()<0.3:
               p=cand; cur=score; no_improve+=1; sigma=max(0.03, sigma*0.99)
           else:
               no_improve+=1
               if no_improve % 100 ==0: sigma=max(0.03, sigma*0.9)
       if no_improve > 2000:
           # reset to best? or increase sigma
           p=best.copy(); cur=best_score; no_improve=0; sigma=1.0
    return best,best_score
```
Use time loop; score_fast each iteration. For n=4096, maybe 1000-5000 iterations.

LP optimizer:
```
def lp_build_A(p):
    n=p.size
    P = p * float(n)
    # optional threshold
    # vectorized arrays
    cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
    rows = cols + np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
    data = np.tile(P, n)
    mask = data > 1e-15
    if not np.all(mask):
       rows=rows[mask]; cols=cols[mask]; data=data[mask]
    A = sp.csr_array((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
    return A
```
Memory: `cols` for n=2048 length 4M int64 32MB, rows another 32, data 32, maybe 100MB; okay? 1 CPU memory unknown maybe limited. Limit LP n<=1024 (1M -> 24MB). Good.

`linprog` with A as csr_array; scipy older expects `scipy.sparse.csr_matrix`. Use `sp.csr_matrix`. `csr_array` may not be accepted? Use `csr_matrix`.
```
def lp_best_response(p, max_time, t0=0.5):
   deadline...
   p=normalize_nonneg(p)
   best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fast(p)
   t=t0
   it=0
   while time < deadline:
      b=fft_conv_same(p); m=max(b)
      if m<=0: break
      # Use constraints p*h <= m or p*n*h <=1? Let's use C = p * n, RHS 1. This shape independent. But if m not used? Suppose p very peaked, entries large; okay.
      if n>1024: maybe still try if n<=2048? Could try if time remaining >120.
      A=build...
      res=linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(2*n-1), bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(5, deadline-now-2), 'output_flag':0})
      if not res.success:
         t*=0.7; continue
      h=res.x
      hs=h.sum()
      if hs <=0: continue
      q=h/hs
      p_new=(1-t)*p + t*q
      p_new=normalize_nonneg(p_new)
      score=score_fast(p_new)
      it++
      if score < best_score - 1e-12:
          best=p_new; best_score=score; p=p_new; t=min(1.0,t*1.05)
      else:
          # maybe try smaller t
          t *=0.7
          if t<0.05: break
   return best,best_score
```
But LP with C=p*n and RHS 1: If p uniform, entries=1, constraints row sums up to n, optimum h perhaps alternating. If p has very small entries, C small, h large. Fine.

Does `b_ub` need to be M? We set p*h≤1. The LP shape independent of M as argued. But is that the exact SOTA? They maximize ∑ g subject to f*g ≤ M. Let h = g/M; constraints f*h ≤1. Yes. Good. We don't use m except to check. If m=0 invalid.

Could there be unbounded h if some column of C all zeros? If p has all zeros? no. If p has first many zeros but positive later, column 0 has C[k,0]=p[k]*n, p[0] may zero but p[1] etc. Since p sum1, some positive, column 0 has positives. Good.

LP objective might be unbounded if C has zero column: column j positive if some p[k-j] positive, always because p has at least one positive; for any j, choose k such that k-j equals positive index within 0..n-1? Need k in 0..2n-2. Since positive index l exists; k=j+l within both ranges. So column positive. Bounded.

LP time may dominate. We can limit to `max_iter=5` and skip if `max_time<60`.

Propose algorithm details:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=t_start+max(10,budget_s)-30
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    candidates=[]
    # parent
    parent_seq = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if parent_seq is not None:
       p=normalize_nonneg(parent_seq)
       if p is not None and p.size>0:
          if p.size <= 8192:
             candidates.append((score_fast(p), p, 'parent'))
           else:
             p2=resample_profile(p, 2048); if ok append
    # choose lengths: [256, 512, 1024, 2048] maybe 4096 if budget large
    lengths=[]
    if budget_s > 1000: lengths=[256,512,1024,2048,4096]
    elif budget_s > 500: lengths=[512,1024,2048]
    else: lengths=[512,1024]
    for n in lengths:
       # deterministic/random profiles
       profs=make_profiles(n, rng, max_profiles=300)
       # parametric search maybe
       param_p, param_score = parametric_search(n, rng, max_time=20)
       if param_p is not None: append
       # score profiles
       for p in profs:
          sc=score_fast(p)
          candidates.append((sc,p,'prof'+n))
       if time > deadline/10 break? don't spend too much.
    # sort keep top 20
    candidates.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
    top=candidates[:20]
    best_p, best_score = top[0]
    # local optimize top candidates
    n_opt_candidates = 3 if budget_s<1000 else 5
    for idx,(score,p,tag) in enumerate(top[:n_opt_candidates]):
       if time_remaining < 120: break
       rem = min(240, time_remaining/ n_opt_candidates) maybe
       p2,sc2=exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p, max_time=rem, rng=rng, name=tag)
       # block polish small
       if time_remaining > 60:
          p3,sc3=block_local_search(p2, max_time=min(60, time_remaining/2), rng=rng)
       else p3,sc3=p2,sc2
       if sc3<best_score: best_p, best_score=p3, sc3
       # add to toplist for exact
    # LP on best maybe
    n=best_p.size
    if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT and n<=1024 and time_remaining>120:
       p_lp, sc_lp = lp_best_response(best_p, max_time=min(300, time_remaining-60), t0=0.5)
       if sc_lp<best_score: best...
       # maybe LP on second
    # Multi-scale upsample best if time
    if time_remaining>300 and best_p.size < 4096:
       target_n = best_p.size*2 if best_p.size*2 <=4096 else 4096
       p_up=resample_profile(best_p,target_n)
       p_up,sc_up=exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p_up,max_time=min(300,time_remaining-100),rng=rng,name='upsample')
       p_up,sc_up=block_local_search(p_up,max_time=min(120,time_remaining-50),rng=rng)
       # maybe exact? keep
       if sc_up<best_score: best...
    # If parent length 3809 and best smaller, maybe upsample best to 3809? Could improve if length larger. Let target_len = parent length if between 2048 and8192.
    if time_remaining>200:
       # choose final target length: max of best_p.size, min(parent_len,4096) maybe
       target = best_p.size
       if parent len exists and 1024<=parent_len<=4096 and parent_len>target: target=parent_len
       if target != best_p.size and target<=4096:
           p_final=resample_profile(best_p,target)
           p_final,sc_final=exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p_final,max_time=min(240,time_remaining-40),rng=rng,name='final_len')
           p_final,sc_final=block_local_search(p_final,max_time=min(80,time_remaining-20),rng=rng)
           if sc_final<best_score: best...
    # exact evaluate top candidates (unique)
    exact_candidates=... best_p plus top list maybe
    best_exact_seq=None; best_exact_val=np.inf
    for p in unique top:
       if time_remaining<2: break
       seq=scale_for_eval(p)
       val=try_evaluate(seq)
       if val<best_exact_val:
          best_exact_val=val; best_exact_seq=seq
    print final
    return best_exact_seq if not None else scale_for_eval(best_p)

`profile_from_params(params, n)`:
```
def profile_from_params(params, n):
    if params.size<5: ...
    alpha = float(params[0]); s1=float(params[1]); phi1=float(params[2]); s2=float(params[3]); phi2=float(params[4])
    # optional s3
    if params.size>5: s3=params[5]; phi3=params[6]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    z = alpha*(x-0.5) + s1*np.cos(2*np.pi*x + phi1) + s2*np.cos(4*np.pi*x + phi2)
    if size>6: z += s3*np.cos(6*np.pi*x + phi3)
    z = np.clip(z, -12, 12)
    v=np.exp(z)
    return normalize_profile(v)
```

```

Manual local param optimization:
- Keep best params; for each param, try small perturbations (randomized coordinate hill climb). This is easy no closures.
```
def parametric_search(n, rng, max_time):
   deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
   best_params = sample_param(rng)
   best_p = profile_from_params(best_params,n); best_score=score_fast(best_p)
   step_scale = 0.5
   no_improve=0
   while time < deadline:
      # full random samples up to e.g. 20% time
      for _ in range(20): sample update
      # coordinate perturbations on best
      dim=best_params.size
      j=int(rng.integers(0,dim))
      candidate_params=best_params.copy()
      # perturb bound-aware
      if j==0: candidate_params[0] += rng.uniform(-1,1)
      elif j in (1,3,5): candidate_params[j] += rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)
      else: candidate_params[j] += rng.uniform(-0.5,0.5)
      clip params to bounds
      p=profile; score
      if improve: update; no_improve=0 else no_improve++
      if no_improve>...: reduce step or new random
   return best_p,best_score
```
This may not be powerful but okay.

Let's write `make_profiles` concretely:
```
def make_profiles(n, rng):
    profs=[]
    xs = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    # deterministic exponential
    for lam in [-2.0,-1.5,-1.0,-0.75,-0.5,-0.25,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5,2.0]:
       append_profile(profs, np.exp(lam*(xs-0.5)))
    # power
    for a in [-1.5,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0]:
       append_profile(profs, (xs+1e-3)**a)
       append_profile(profs, ((1.0-xs)+1e-3)**a)
    # sinusoidal nonnegative
    for d in [0, np.pi/4, np.pi/2, 3*np.pi/4]:
       append_profile(profs, 1.0 + 0.8*np.cos(2*np.pi*xs + d))
       append_profile(profs, 1.0 + 0.8*np.sin(2*np.pi*xs + d))
    # exp * cos
    for lam in [-1.0,0.0,1.0,2.0]:
       base=np.exp(lam*(xs-0.5))
       for d in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi, 3*np.pi/2]:
          for amp in [0.4,0.8,1.0]:
             append_profile(profs, base*(1.0+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*xs+d)))
    # random log-linear
    count=0
    max_rand=120
    while len(profs)<max_rand and count<max_rand*5:
       count+=1
       z = np.zeros(n)
       if rng.random()<0.8:
          lam=rng.uniform(-3.0,3.0)
          z += lam*(xs-0.5)
       for m, max_amp in [(1,2.5),(2,2.0),(3,1.5)]:
          if rng.random()<0.8:
             amp=rng.uniform(0.0,max_amp)
             if rng.random()<0.5: amp=-amp
             phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
             if rng.random()<0.5:
                z += amp*np.cos(m*2*np.pi*xs + phase)
             else:
                z += amp*np.sin(m*2*np.pi*xs)
       append_profile(profs, np.exp(np.clip(z,-12,12)))
    # gaussian mixtures
    for means in ([0.2,0.8],[0.3,0.7],[0.1,0.5,0.9],[0.25,0.6,0.85]):
       for widths in ([0.1,0.3],[0.2,0.2],[0.15,0.25,0.3]):
          if len(means)!=len(widths): continue
          for wts in ([0.3,0.7],[0.7,0.3],[0.5,0.5],[0.2,0.6,0.2]):
             if len(wts)!=len(widths): continue
             v=np.zeros(n)
             for c,w,wt in zip(means,widths,wts):
                v += wt*np.exp(-0.5*((xs-c)/w)**2)
             append_profile(profs, v+1e-6)
    # random smooth no clip
    for _ in range(50):
       z = rng.normal(0,0.3,n)
       # smooth by convolution with small kernel
       kwin = max(1, n//200)
       kern = np.exp(-np.arange(-kwin,kwin+1)**2/(2*(kwin/3)**2)); kern/=kern.sum()
       z = np.convolve(z,kern,mode='same')
       append_profile(profs, np.exp(np.clip(z,-10,10)))
    return profs
```

Detailed top-level functions:

```
def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`fft_conv_same`:
```
def fft_conv_same(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    ca = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return ca[:L]
```
Could use `np.real` not needed.

`fft_crosscorr_p_weights`:
```
def fft_crosscorr_p_weights(p, u):
    n = p.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    if u.size < L:
        up = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        up[:u.size] = u
        u = up
    N = next_pow2(n + u.size - 1)
    pr = p[::-1]
    fr = np.fft.rfft(pr, N)
    fu = np.fft.rfft(u, N)
    g = np.fft.irfft(fr * fu, N)
    return g[n-1:n-1+n]
```

`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=1e-12):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return np.ones(1)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(a) / a.size
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    a /= a.sum()
    return a
```
Maybe floor absolute 1e-12 can dominate if n huge >1e12? n small. Good.

`normalize_profile` similar but returns None if invalid size? Could reuse:
```
def normalize_profile(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).ravel()
    v=np.nan_to_num(...)
    v=np.maximum(v,0)
    if v.size==0: return None
    s=v.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return None
    return v/s
```
No floor to preserve zeros? For profiles, zeros okay. But later normalize_nonneg adds floor. Use profile without floor for starts; score handles zeros? If p has zeros, FFT okay; gradient may divide? EG uses p factors; zeros stay zero due p* factors, floor after. Start with zeros may limit support; but okay. Maybe use floor in normalize_nonneg during optimizer.

`score_fast`: if a has zeros, sum okay. If max b negative due round, set 0. Need maybe clip small negative in b? Max positive.
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0, posinf=0, neginf=0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=1e-300: return np.inf
    a=a/s
    b=fft_conv_same(a)
    m=float(np.max(b))
    if not finite: return inf
    if m <0: m=0
    return float(2*a.size*m)
```

`scale_for_eval`:
```
def scale_for_eval(p):
    p=npp? use np.asarray
    p=np.nan_to_num(np.maximum(p,0),...)
    mx=float(np.max(p))
    if mx <= 0 or not finite:
       return [1.0]*max(1,p.size)
    out=p*(1000.0/mx)
    out=np.clip(out,0.0,1000.0)
    if out.size and float(np.sum(out)) < 0.01:
       # raise extremely tiny by adding small uniform within 1000
       out = np.maximum(out, 1e-6)
       out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)
    # ensure list floats
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
If p length 1, out=1000, sum 1000.

`try_evaluate`:
```
def try_evaluate(seq):
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
    try:
       val=float(val)
    except Exception:
       return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val): return np.inf
    return val
```

`resample_profile`:
```
def resample_profile(p, new_n):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).ravel()
    if p.size <=0: return None
    if p.size == int(new_n): return normalize_profile(p)
    old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size)
    new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,int(new_n))
    q=np.interp(new, old, p)
    q=np.maximum(q,0.0)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    return q/s
```

`append_profile`:
```
def append_profile(profs, v):
    p = normalize_profile(v)
    if p is not None:
        profs.append(p)
```

`random_block_perturb` as above. Need if width >= n, set width=n-1? Ensure n-width positive.
```
def random_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.5):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).copy()
    n=p.size
    if n <= 4:
       p=np.maximum(p,1e-12); p/=p.sum(); return p
    lo=max(2, int(n//1000))
    hi=max(lo+2, min(n-1, int(n//4)))
    width=int(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(lo), np.log(hi))))
    width=max(1,min(n-1,width))
    start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
    r=rng.random()
    if r < 0.55:
       p[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width))
    elif r < 0.75:
       p[start:start+width] = 0.0
    else:
       src=int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
       p[start:start+width] = p[src:src+width]
    p=np.clip(p,1e-12,None)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return normalize_nonneg(p)
    p/=s
    return p
```
If p[src] and start overlap, assignment uses slice; okay.

`exponentiated_gradient_optimize`: Need pass rng for restarts. Implement with deadline. Use `time.monotonic()`.
```
def exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p0, max_time, rng):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p0)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fast(p)
    cur=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    step_target=1.0
    beta_range=8.0
    no_improve=0
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    it=0
    restart_count=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        b=fft_conv_same(cur)
        m=float(np.max(b))
        if not finite or m<=1e-100: break
        beta=beta_range/m
        wb=np.exp(beta*(b-m))
        sw=wb.sum()
        if not finite or sw<=0:
           k=int(min(b.size,64))
           idx=np.argsort(b)[-k:]
           wb=np.zeros(b.size); wb[idx]=1.0; sw=float(k)
        u=wb/sw
        grad=2.0*fft_crosscorr_p_weights(cur,u)
        grad=grad - float(np.mean(grad))
        scale=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if not finite or scale<=1e-15:
           # random perturb and continue
           new=random_block_perturb(best,rng,1.0)
           new_score=score_fast(new)
           if new_score<best_score:
              best=new; best_score=new_score
           cur=new; cur_score=score_fast(cur)
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve>50: break
           continue
        eta=step_target/scale
        logfac=-eta*grad
        logfac=np.clip(logfac,-3.0,3.0)
        cur_new=cur*np.exp(logfac)
        cur_new=np.maximum(cur_new,1e-12)
        cur_new/=cur_new.sum()
        sc=score_fast(cur_new)
        it+=1
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=cur_new.copy(); best_score=sc
           cur=cur_new.copy(); cur_score=sc
           no_improve=0
           step_target=min(2.5, step_target*1.05)
        else:
           # Accept if not much worse than current
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score + max(0.001, 0.001*cur_score):
               cur=cur_new.copy(); cur_score=sc
           else:
               if no_improve > 30:
                   cur=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
               # else keep current? cur_new rejected, cur stays
           step_target=max(0.2, step_target*0.98)
           no_improve+=1
        if no_improve > 600:
           # restart from best with perturbation
           cur=random_block_perturb(best,rng,1.0)
           cur_score=score_fast(cur)
           no_improve=0
           step_target=1.5
           restart_count+=1
          print(f"EG restart {restart_count} score {best_score:.6f}")
        if it % 500 == 0:
           print(f"EG it {it} best {best_score:.6f}")
    return best, best_score
```
One issue: gradient computed from cur, but if we reject and cur remains old, next loop recomputes. Fine. If no_improve and cur=best, okay.

Beta_range 8 maybe too flat? Use 12 for focus. Let's choose 12. Clip exponent [-4,4] maybe. Step target start 1.0. Good.

`block_local_search`: Use `random_block_perturb`; maybe include exact? fast.
```
def block_local_search(p0, max_time, rng):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p0)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fast(p); cur=p.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    sigma=0.4
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       cand=random_block_perturb(cur, rng, sigma)
       sc=score_fast(cand)
       it +=1
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
          best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc; cur=cand.copy(); cur_score=sc; no_improve=0; sigma=min(1.5,sigma*1.02)
       else:
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score + max(0.0005,0.0005*cur_score) and rng.random()<0.4:
             cur=cand.copy(); cur_score=sc; no_improve+=1; sigma=max(0.05,sigma*0.995)
          else:
             no_improve+=1
             if no_improve % 200 ==0:
                 sigma=max(0.05,sigma*0.9)
          if no_improve > 3000:
             cur=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score; no_improve=0; sigma=1.0
       if it % 200 ==0:
          print(f"BLK it {it} best {best_score:.6f}")
    return best,best_score
```

LP optimizer:

def build_lp_matrix(p):
    n=p.size
    P=p*float(n)
    # Use arrays; for n <= 1536 maybe
    cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
    rows = cols + np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
    data = np.tile(P, n)
    mask = data > 1e-14
    if not np.all(mask):
       rows=rows[mask]; cols=cols[mask]; data=data[mask]
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*n-1, n))
    return A
```
Memory for n=2048 ~4M arrays; maybe okay but 100MB. Limit to n<=1536? Use if budget and n <= 1200 maybe. We can choose 1024. If best n=2048, skip LP or downsample best to 1024 for LP? The SOTA could be applied at 1024 and upsample. But maybe not.

def candidate_score(item):
    return item[0]

def add_candidate(pool, score, p):
    if p is not None and np.isfinite(score):
        pool.append((score, np.asarray(p,float), len(pool)))
```

In `propose_candidate`:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=max(10.0,float(budget_s))
    deadline=start + max(10.0, budget - 30.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pool=[]
    print("Starting optimize budget", budget)
    # parent
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    parent_len=0
    if parent is not None:
       p=normalize_profile(parent)
       if p is not None:
          parent_len=p.size
          if p.size <= 8192:
             sc=score_fast(p); add_candidate(pool, sc, p)
             print(f"parent score {sc:.6f} len {p.size}")
          else:
             p2=resample_profile(p, 2048)
             if p2 is not None:
                sc=score_fast(p2); add_candidate(pool, sc, p2)
    # lengths
    if budget > 1800:
       lengths=[256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096]
    elif budget > 900:
       lengths=[256, 512, 1024, 2048]
    else:
       lengths=[512, 1024]
    # But don't spend too long in profiling for many large lengths. Use per-length time.
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 600: break  # leave time for optimization
       profs=make_profiles(n, rng)
       for p in profs:
          sc=score_fast(p)
          add_candidate(pool, sc, p)

`profile_fourier`:
```
def profile_fourier(coef, n):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    alpha=np.clip(coef[0], -3,3)
    z=alpha*(x-0.5)
    max谐波= (len(coef)-1)//2
    for m in range(1,max谐波+1):
       a=coef[2*m-1]; b=coef[2*m]
       if a!=0: z += a*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x)
       if b!=0: z += b*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x)
    z=np.clip(z,-12,12)
    return normalize_profile(np.exp(z))
```
Bounds for coeff: linear [-3,3], cos/sin [-2.5,2.5] for m=1, [-2,2] m=2, [-1.5] m=3. We can define top-level `clip_fourier_coef(coef)`.

class FourierMinimizer:

        if p is None: return 1e30
        return score_fast(p)
```

Implement `fourier_search`:
```
def fourier_search(n, rng, max_time):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT: return random profile? 
    D=4; dim=1+2*D
    opt=FourierMinimizer(n)
    best_p=None; best_sc=np.inf
    # initial candidates
    x0s=[]
    x0=np.zeros(dim); x0[0]=2.0; x0s.append(x0)
    x0=np.zeros(dim); x0[1]=2.0; x0[2]=-1.0; x0s.append(x0)
    for _ in range(6):
       x=np.zeros(dim)
       x[0]=rng.uniform(-3,3)
       for j in range(1,dim): x[j]=rng.uniform(-2,2)
       x0s.append(x)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    for x0 in x0s:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2: break
       res = minimize(opt.fun, x0, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter': 300, 'xatol':0.02, 'fatol':1e-5, 'adaptive': True})
       xres=np.asarray(res.x)
       p=profile_fourier(xres,n)
       if p is not None:
          sc=score_fast(p)
          if sc<best_sc: best_p=p; best_sc=sc
    return best_p,best_sc
```
Need import `minimize` from scipy.optimize if available. Top-level flag.

`FourierMinimizer.fun` calls `score_fast`, which uses FFT; many evaluations. Nelder-Mead 300 iterations * 9 dims ~2700 evals per initial, 7 inits ~19k evals. For n=512, maybe seconds; for n=2048, slower. We can limit: run fourier_search only for n in [512,1024] and max_time ~30s. Use as additional initial profiles.

`scale_for_eval`: If p empty, return [1.0]? length 0 invalid. Return [1.0]. But p should not empty.
```
def scale_for_eval(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).ravel()
    if p.size==0:
       return [1.0,1.0]
    p=np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    mx=float(np.max(p))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <=0:
       return [1.0]*p.size
    scale=1000.0/(mx*(1.0+1e-12))
    out=p*scale
    out=np.minimum(out,1000.0)
    if float(out.sum()) < 0.01:
       out=np.maximum(out, 1e-6)
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
If p size huge, max positive. Good.

```
def clip_fourier_coef(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).copy()
    x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    x[0]=np.clip(x[0], -3.0, 3.0)
    for j in range(1,x.size):
       amp = 2.5 / (0.5 + j)
       x[j]=np.clip(x[j], -amp, amp)
    return x
```
Use in profile.

`fourier_search`: If no scipy, generate a few random profiles via `profile_fourier` and return best. Code:
```
def fourier_search(n, rng, max_time):
    D=4; dim=1+2*D
    x0_list=[]
    # deterministic
    base=np.zeros(dim); base[0]=1.5; x0_list.append(base.copy())
    base[0]=-1.5; x0_list.append(base.copy())
    base=np.zeros(dim); base[1]=1.5; base[2]=-0.7; x0_list.append(base.copy())
    for _ in range(5): ...
    opt=FourierMinimizer(n)
    best_p=None; best_sc=np.inf
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    for x0 in x0_list:
       if time.monotonic()>deadline-2: break
       x0=clip_fourier_coef(x0)
       if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT:
           try:
              res=minimize(opt.fun, x0, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':250,'xatol':0.02,'fatol':1e-5,'adaptive':True})
              xr=clip_fourier_coef(res.x)
           except Exception:
              xr=x0
       else:
          xr=x0
       p=profile_fourier(xr,n)
       if p is not None:
          sc=score_fast(p); update
       # if time, a few random restart? okay
    return best_p,best_sc
```

def add_candidate(pool, score, p):
    if p is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    pool.append((float(score), p, len(pool)))
```

    try: ... return A
    except Exception: return None
```

Now, final code. Let's produce. Ensure only one fenced code block. final answer.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing

    2*n*max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)**2

using FFT-based surrogate scoring, exponentiated-gradient local search
with smoothed max coefficients, LP best-response refinement from the prior
state-of-the-art idea, and block perturbations.  The final answer is selected
by direct calls to the official evaluate_sequence() when available.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    from scipy.optimize import linprog, minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY_OPT = False


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fft_conv_same(a):
    """Exact linear convolution of a with itself, via zero-padded FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    ca = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return ca[:L]


def fft_crosscorr_p_weights(p, u):
    """
    For p length n and coefficient weights u length >= 2n-1, compute
    g_i = sum_l p_l u_{i+l}, i=0..n-1.
    """
    n = p.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if u.size < L:
        up = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        up[:u.size] = u
        u = up
    N = next_pow2(n + u.size - 1)
    pr = p[::-1]
    fr = np.fft.rfft(pr, N)
    fu = np.fft.rfft(u, N)
    g = np.fft.irfft(fr * fu, N)
    return g[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def normalize_profile(v):
    """Normalize a nonnegative profile to sum one; return None if invalid."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if v.size == 0:
        return None
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return v / s


def normalize_nonneg(a, floor=1e-12):
    """Normalize a vector to the probability simplex with a tiny positive floor."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    a = np.maximum(a, float(floor))
    a = a / float(a.sum())
    return a


def append_profile(profs, v):
    p = normalize_profile(v)
    if p is not None:
        profs.append(p)


def score_fast(a):
    """Fast normalized objective: 2*n*max(conv(a,a)) with sum(a)=1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    a = a / s
    b = fft_conv_same(a)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * a.size * m)


def resample_profile(p, new_n):
    """Linearly resample a normalized profile to a new length."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size <= 0:
        return None
    new_n = int(max(1, new_n))
    if p.size == new_n:
        return normalize_profile(p)
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n)
    q = np.interp(new, old, p)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return q / s


def scale_for_eval(p):
    """Scale a normalized sequence so its maximum is safely below 1000."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return [1.0, 1.0]
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    mx = float(np.max(p))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
        return [1.0] * p.size
    scale = 1000.0 / (mx * (1.0 + 1e-12))
    out = p * scale
    out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)
    if float(out.sum()) < 0.01:
        out = np.maximum(out, 1e-6)
    return [float(x) for x in out]


def try_evaluate(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return np.inf
    return val


def random_block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.5):
    """Random localized nonnegative perturbation of a normalized vector."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = p.size
    if n <= 4:
        p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
        s = float(p.sum())
        if s <= 0.0:
            return normalize_nonneg(p)
        return p / s

    lo = max(2, int(n // 1000))
    hi = max(lo + 2, min(n - 1, int(n // 4)))
    width = int(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(lo), np.log(hi))))
    width = max(1, min(n - 1, width))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width))

    r = float(rng.random())
    if r < 0.55:
        jitter = np.clip(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=width), -5.0, 5.0)
        p[start:start + width] *= np.exp(jitter)
    elif r < 0.75:
        p[start:start + width] = 0.0
    else:
        src = int(rng.integers(0, n - width))
        p[start:start + width] = p[src:src + width]

    p = np.clip(p, 1e-12, None)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return normalize_nonneg(p)
    return p / s


def exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p0, max_time, rng):
    """
    Exponentiated-gradient / smoothed-max descent on the simplex.
    The objective is scale-invariant, so we keep sum(p)=1 and minimize
    2*n*max(p*p).
    """
    p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(p)
    cur = p.copy()
    cur_score = best_score

    step_target = 1.0
    beta_range = 12.0
    no_improve = 0
    restarts = 0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max(1.0, max_time))
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        b = fft_conv_same(cur)
        m = float(np.max(b))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 1e-100:
            break

        beta = beta_range / m
        wb = np.exp(beta * (b - m))
        sw = float(wb.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
            k = int(min(b.size, 64))
            idx = np.argsort(b)[-k:]
            wb = np.zeros(b.size, dtype=np.float64)
            wb[idx] = 1.0
            sw = float(max(1, k))
        u = wb / sw

        grad = 2.0 * fft_crosscorr_p_weights(cur, u)
        grad = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))

        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 1e-15:
            trial = random_block_perturb(best, rng, sigma=1.0)
            ts = score_fast(trial)
            if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < best_score:
                best = trial.copy()
                best_score = ts
            cur = trial.copy()
            cur_score = score_fast(cur)
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 50:
                break
            continue

        eta = step_target / scale
        logfac = -eta * grad
        logfac = np.clip(logfac, -4.0, 4.0)
        cur_new = cur * np.exp(logfac)
        cur_new = np.maximum(cur_new, 1e-12)
        cur_new = cur_new / float(cur_new.sum())
        sc = score_fast(cur_new)
        it += 1

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cur_new.copy()
            best_score = sc
            cur = cur_new.copy()
            cur_score = sc
            no_improve = 0
            step_target = min(2.5, step_target * 1.05)
        else:
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score + max(0.001, 0.001 * max(1.0, cur_score)):
                cur = cur_new.copy()
                cur_score = sc
                no_improve += 1
            else:
                if no_improve > 30:
                    cur = best.copy()
                    cur_score = best_score
                no_improve += 1
            step_target = max(0.2, step_target * 0.98)

        if no_improve > 600:
            cur = random_block_perturb(best, rng, sigma=1.2)
            cur_score = score_fast(cur)
            no_improve = 0
            step_target = 1.6
            restarts += 1
            print(f"EG restart {restarts}, best {best_score:.6f}")

        if it % 500 == 0:
            print(f"EG iter {it}, fast best {best_score:.6f}")

    return best, best_score


def block_local_search(p0, max_time, rng):
    """Hill-climbing local search by random block perturbations."""
    p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(p)
    cur = p.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    sigma = 0.4
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max(1.0, max_time))

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = random_block_perturb(cur, rng, sigma)
        sc = score_fast(cand)
        it += 1

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand.copy()
            best_score = sc
            cur = cand.copy()
            cur_score = sc
            no_improve = 0
            sigma = min(1.5, sigma * 1.02)
        else:
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score + max(0.0005, 0.0005 * max(1.0, cur_score)):
                if float(rng.random()) < 0.4:
                    cur = cand.copy()
                    cur_score = sc
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 200 == 0:
                sigma = max(0.05, sigma * 0.9)
            if no_improve > 3000:
                cur = best.copy()
                cur_score = best_score
                no_improve = 0
                sigma = 1.0

        if it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"Block iter {it}, fast best {best_score:.6f}")

    return best, best_score


def build_lp_matrix(p):
    """
    Build the (2n-1) x n convolution matrix A with A[k, j] = n*p[k-j]
    if 0 <= k-j < n.  This is the LP matrix for the best-response step.
    """
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    try:
        P = p * float(n)
        cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
        rows = cols + np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
        data = np.tile(P, n)
        mask = data > 1e-14
        if not np.all(mask):
            rows = rows[mask]
            cols = cols[mask]
            data = data[mask]
        if data.size == 0:
            return None
        A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
        return A
    except Exception:
        return None


def lp_best_response(p0, max_time, t0=0.4):
    """
    LP-based best response:
        maximize sum(h) subject to p*h <= 1, h >= 0,
    then move toward h/sum(h).
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY_OPT:
        return p0, score_fast(p0)

    p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n < 16 or n > 1200:
        return p, score_fast(p)

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(p)
    t = float(t0)
    no_progress = 0
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max(2.0, max_time))
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        A = build_lp_matrix(p)
        if A is None:
            break

        remaining = max(1.0, float(deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0))
        time_limit = int(max(1, min(3600, remaining)))
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

        try:
            res = linprog(
                -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64),
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=bounds,
                method='highs',
                options={'time_limit': time_limit, 'output_flag': 0}
            )
        except Exception:
            try:
                res = linprog(
                    -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64),
                    A_ub=A,
                    b_ub=b_ub,
                    bounds=bounds,
                    method='interior-point',
                    options={'finpack': True, 'maxiter': 500}
                )
            except Exception:
                break

        if not getattr(res, 'success', False):
            t *= 0.7
            no_progress += 1
            if t < 0.05 or no_progress > 8:
                break
            continue

        h = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        hs = float(h.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(hs) or hs <= 0.0:
            t *= 0.7
            no_progress += 1
            if t < 0.05 or no_progress > 8:
                break
            continue

        q = h / hs
        p_new = (1.0 - t) * p + t * q
        p_new = normalize_nonneg(p_new)
        sc = score_fast(p_new)
        it += 1

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = p_new.copy()
            best_score = sc
            p = p_new.copy()
            t = min(0.9, t * 1.1)
            no_progress = 0
            print(f"LP iter {it}, fast best {best_score:.6f}")
        else:
            t *= 0.7
            no_progress += 1
            if t < 0.05 or no_progress > 10:
                break

    return best, best_score


def profile_fourier(coef, n):
    """
    Positive profile of the form exp(alpha*x + Fourier modes), clipped and
    normalized.
    """
    x = np.asarray(coef, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    n = int(max(1, n))
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size > 0:
        alpha = float(np.clip(x[0], -3.0, 3.0))
        xx = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        z += alpha * (xx - 0.5)
    for m in range(1, max(1, (x.size - 1) // 2 + 1)):
        a = float(np.clip(x[2 * m - 1], -3.0 / float(m), 3.0 / float(m)))
        b = float(np.clip(x[2 * m], -3.0 / float(m), 3.0 / float(m)))
        if a != 0.0:
            z += a * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * xx)
        if b != 0.0:
            z += b * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * xx)
    z = np.clip(z, -12.0, 12.0)
    return normalize_profile(np.exp(z))


class FourierMinimizer:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = int(n)

    def fun(self, x):
        p = profile_fourier(x, self.n)
        if p is None:
            return 1e30
        return float(score_fast(p))


def fourier_search(n, rng, max_time):
    """Low-dimensional Fourier search to produce strong starts."""
    D = 4
    dim = 1 + 2 * D
    n = int(max(32, n))
    opt = FourierMinimizer(n)

    x0s = []
    x0 = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
    x0[0] = 1.8
    x0s.append(x0.copy())

    x0 = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
    x0[0] = -1.8
    x0s.append(x0.copy())

    x0 = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
    x0[0] = 0.8
    x0[1] = 1.6
    x0[2] = -0.9
    x0[3] = 0.9
    x0[4] = 0.4
    x0s.append(x0.copy())

    x0 = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
    x0[0] = 2.2
    x0[1] = -1.4
    x0[2] = 1.2
    x0[5] = 0.8
    x0[6] = -0.4
    x0s.append(x0.copy())

    for _ in range(6):
        x0 = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float64)
        x0[0] = float(rng.uniform(-3.0, 3.0))
        for j in range(1, dim):
            amp = 2.5 / float(0.5 + j)
            x0[j] = float(rng.uniform(-amp, amp))
        x0s.append(x0)

    best_p = None
    best_sc = np.inf
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max(2.0, max_time))

    for x0 in x0s:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 1.5:
            break
        x0 = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64)
        if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT:
            try:
                res = minimize(
                    opt.fun,
                    x0,
                    method='Nelder-Mead',
                    options={'maxiter': 260, 'xatol': 0.02, 'fatol': 1e-5}
                )
                xr = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            except Exception:
                xr = x0
        else:
            xr = x0

        p = profile_fourier(xr, n)
        if p is not None:
            sc = score_fast(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_p = p
                best_sc = sc

    if best_p is None:
        p = profile_fourier(x0s[0], n)
        if p is not None:
            best_p = p
            best_sc = score_fast(p)

    return best_p, best_sc


def make_profiles(n, rng):
    """Generate a diversified set of feasible normalized profiles."""
    n = int(max(16, n))
    xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    profs = []

    # Simple monotone skewings.
    for lam in (-2.5, -2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.75, -0.5, -0.25,
                0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5):
        append_profile(profs, np.exp(lam * (xs - 0.5)))

    # Power laws at ends.
    for a in (-2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.75, -0.5, -0.25,
              0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0):
        append_profile(profs, (xs + 1e-3) ** a)
        append_profile(profs, ((1.0 - xs) + 1e-3) ** a)

    # Stable trigonometric nonnegative profiles.
    for d in (0.0, np.pi / 4, np.pi / 2, 3.0 * np.pi / 4,
              np.pi, 5.0 * np.pi / 4, 3.0 * np.pi / 2):
        append_profile(profs, 1.0 + 0.8 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * xs + d))
        append_profile(profs, 1.0 + 0.8 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * xs + d))

    # Exponential times cosine, a useful mixed family.
    for lam in (-2.0, -1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0):
        base = np.exp(lam * (xs - 0.5))
        for d in (0.0, np.pi / 2, np.pi, 3.0 * np.pi / 2):
            for amp in (0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.0):
                append_profile(profs, base * (1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * xs + d)))

    # Gaussian mixtures.
    mixture_sets = [
        ([0.2, 0.8], [0.1, 0.3], [0.3, 0.7]),
        ([0.2, 0.8], [0.2, 0.2], [0.7, 0.3]),
        ([0.3, 0.7], [0.15, 0.25], [0.5, 0.5]),
        ([0.25, 0.6, 0.85], [0.12, 0.22, 0.28], [0.2, 0.6, 0.2]),
        ([0.1, 0.5, 0.9], [0.15, 0.2, 0.3], [0.5, 0.3, 0.2]),
    ]
    for means, widths, wts in mixture_sets:
        if len(means) != len(widths) or len(wts) != len(widths):
            continue
        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for c, w, wt in zip(means, widths, wts):
            v += wt * np.exp(-0.5 * ((xs - c) / w) ** 2)
        append_profile(profs, v + 1e-6)

    # Random log-linear smooth profiles.
    for _ in range(140):
        z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        if float(rng.random()) < 0.85:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(-3.5, 3.5))
            z += lam * (xs - 0.5)
        for m, max_amp in ((1, 2.6), (2, 2.0), (3, 1.4), (4, 1.0)):
            if float(rng.random()) < 0.75:
                amp = float(rng.uniform(0.0, max_amp))
                if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
                    amp = -amp
                if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
                    phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
                    z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * xs + phase)
                else:
                    amp2 = amp
                    z += amp2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * xs)
        append_profile(profs, np.exp(np.clip(z, -12.0, 12.0)))

    # Random smooth positive curves.
    kwin = max(1, int(n // 200))
    kernel = np.exp(-np.arange(-kwin, kwin + 1) ** 2 / (2.0 * max(1e-6, kwin / 3.0) ** 2))
    kernel = kernel / float(kernel.sum())
    for _ in range(60):
        z = float(0.5) * (float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)) * xs) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.6, n)
        if kwin > 1:
            z = np.convolve(z, kernel, mode='same')
        append_profile(profs, np.exp(np.clip(z, -10.0, 10.0)))

    return profs


def candidate_score(item):
    return item[0]


def add_candidate(pool, score, p):
    if p is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    pool.append((float(score), p, len(pool)))


def prune_pool(pool, keep):
    if len(pool) > keep:
        pool.sort(key=candidate_score)
        del pool[keep:]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.  Returns a list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0:
        budget = 2400.0

    if budget > 1200:
        reserved = 210.0
    else:
        reserved = 30.0
    deadline = start + max(10.0, budget - reserved)
    hard_deadline = start + max(10.0, budget) - 5.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pool = []

    # Fallback uniform candidates.
    for fn in (256, 512, 1024):
        add_candidate(pool, 2.0, np.ones(fn, dtype=np.float64) / float(fn))

    # Parent / known construction.
    parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    parent_len = 0
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            raw = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if raw.size > 200000:
                idx_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, raw.size)
                idx_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 2048)
                raw = np.interp(idx_new, idx_old, raw)
            p = normalize_profile(raw)
            if p is not None:
                parent_len = p.size
                if 1 <= p.size <= 8192:
                    sc = score_fast(p)
                    add_candidate(pool, sc, p)
                    print(f"Parent len={p.size}, fast={sc:.6f}")
                else:
                    p2 = resample_profile(p, 2048)
                    if p2 is not None:
                        sc = score_fast(p2)
                        add_candidate(pool, sc, p2)
                        print(f"Parent resampled len=2048, fast={sc:.6f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    if budget <= 60:
        lengths = [128, 256]
    elif budget <= 300:
        lengths = [256, 512]
    elif budget <= 900:
        lengths = [256, 512, 1024]
    elif budget <= 1800:
        lengths = [256, 512, 1024, 2048]
    else:
        lengths = [256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096]

    # Generate and score starting profiles.
    for n in lengths:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline - 420.0:
            break
        keep_profiles = min(700, max(160, int(22000000 / max(64, n))))
        profs = make_profiles(n, rng)
        if len(profs) > keep_profiles:
            profs = profs[:keep_profiles]

        for p in profs:
            sc = score_fast(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, sc, p)

        prune_pool(pool, 360)

        if n == 512 and budget > 700:
            fp, fs = fourier_search(512, rng, 25.0)
            if fp is not None:
                add_candidate(pool, fs, fp)
        if n == 1024 and budget > 1000:
            fp, fs = fourier_search(1024, rng, 45.0)
            if fp is not None:
                add_candidate(pool, fs, fp)
        if n == 2048 and budget > 1800:
            fp, fs = fourier_search(2048, rng, 60.0)
            if fp is not None:
                add_candidate(pool, fs, fp)

    pool.sort(key=candidate_score)
    best_p = None
    best_score = np.inf
    if pool:
        best_score = pool[0][0]
        best_p = pool[0][1].copy()
    else:
        best_p = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        best_score = 2.0
        add_candidate(pool, best_score, best_p)

    print(f"Initial fast best={best_score:.6f}, len={best_p.size}")

    # Local optimization of a few top starting candidates.
    top_count = 6 if budget > 1200 else 4
    selected = pool[:min(top_count, len(pool))]
    local_budget = min(1200.0, max(180.0, float(budget) * 0.42))
    per = local_budget / max(1, len(selected))

    for idx, item in enumerate(selected):
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline - 620.0:
            break
        max_chunk = min(per, max(20.0, deadline - now - 620.0))
        if max_chunk < 30.0:
            max_chunk = min(per, (deadline - now) * 0.25)
            if max_chunk < 20.0:
                continue

        p1, sc1 = exponentiated_gradient_optimize(item[1], max(5.0, max_chunk * 0.72), rng)
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 300.0:
            blk_t = min(75.0, max(10.0, max_chunk * 0.25))
            if blk_t > 10.0:
                p1, sc1 = block_local_search(p1, blk_t, rng)

        if np.isfinite(sc1):
            add_candidate(pool, sc1, p1)
            if sc1 < best_score:
                best_score = sc1
                best_p = p1.copy()
        print(f"Local opt {idx}: fast={sc1:.6f}, global fast={best_score:.6f}")

    pool.sort(key=candidate_score)

    # LP best-response on a reasonably sized candidate.
    if HAVE_SCIPY_OPT and time.monotonic() < deadline - 260.0:
        lp_p = None
        for item in pool:
            ptmp = item[1]
            if 128 <= ptmp.size <= 1200:
                lp_p = ptmp
                break
        if lp_p is not None:
            lp_t = min(420.0, max(60.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.32))
            try:
                p_lp, sc_lp = lp_best_response(lp_p, lp_t, t0=0.4)
                if np.isfinite(sc_lp):
                    add_candidate(pool, sc_lp, p_lp)
                    if sc_lp < best_score:
                        best_score = sc_lp
                        best_p = p_lp.copy()
                        print(f"LP improved: fast={sc_lp:.6f}")
            except Exception:
                pass

        # Try one more LP candidate if useful.
        done_size = None if best_p is None else best_p.size
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 220.0:
            for item in pool:
                ptmp = item[1]
                if 128 <= ptmp.size <= 1200 and ptmp.size != done_size:
                    lp_t2 = min(220.0, max(40.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.22))
                    if lp_t2 > 40.0:
                        try:
                            p_lp2, sc_lp2 = lp_best_response(
                                ptmp, lp_t2, t0=0.35
                            )
                            if np.isfinite(sc_lp2):
                                add_candidate(pool, sc_lp2, p_lp2)
                                if sc_lp2 < best_score:
                                    best_score = sc_lp2
                                    best_p = p_lp2.copy()
                                    print(f"LP second improved: fast={sc_lp2:.6f}")
                        except Exception:
                            pass
                    break

    pool.sort(key=candidate_score)

    # Multi-scale refinement.
    target_n = int(best_p.size)
    wanted_targets = []
    if budget > 1200:
        if target_n < 2048:
            wanted_targets.append(2048)
        if target_n < 4096:
            wanted_targets.append(4096)
    if budget > 2000 and target_n < 8192:
        wanted_targets.append(8192)
    if parent_len and 1024 <= parent_len <= 8192 and parent_len > target_n:
        wanted_targets.append(min(parent_len, 8192))

    # Stable ascending unique targets.
    uniq_targets = []
    for t in wanted_targets:
        if t > target_n and t not in uniq_targets:
            uniq_targets.append(t)
    uniq_targets.sort()

    for target in uniq_targets:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now > deadline - 260.0:
            break
        p_up = resample_profile(best_p, target)
        if p_up is None:
            continue
        t_alloc = min(360.0, max(60.0, (deadline - now) * 0.32))
        t_grad = min(t_alloc * 0.72, 300.0)
        t_blk = min(t_alloc * 0.25, 90.0)
        if t_grad > 45.0:
            p_up, sc_up = exponentiated_gradient_optimize(p_up, t_grad, rng)
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 160.0 and t_blk > 20.0:
            p_up, sc_up2 = block_local_search(p_up, t_blk, rng)
            sc_up = min(sc_up, sc_up2)
        if np.isfinite(sc_up):
            add_candidate(pool, sc_up, p_up)
            if sc_up < best_score:
                best_score = sc_up
                best_p = p_up.copy()
                print(f"Multi-scale {target}: fast={sc_up:.6f}")

    pool.sort(key=candidate_score)

    # Final block search on the best sequence.
    exact_reserve = 150.0 if budget > 1000 else 40.0
    blk_time = deadline - time.monotonic() - exact_reserve
    if blk_time > 60.0:
        best_p, best_score = block_local_search(best_p, blk_time, rng)
        add_candidate(pool, best_score, best_p)
        print(f"Final block best fast={best_score:.6f}, len={best_p.size}")

    # Exact evaluation of the best surrogate candidates.
    pool.sort(key=candidate_score)
    best_seq = None
    best_exact = np.inf
    exact_left = hard_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if exact_left > 5.0:
        exact_deadline = time.monotonic() + min(180.0, max(10.0, exact_left * 0.92))
        checked = 0
        for item in pool:
            if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline:
                break
            if checked >= 8:
                break
            p = item[1]
            seq = scale_for_eval(p)
            val = try_evaluate(seq)
            checked += 1
            print(f"Exact candidate fast={item[0]:.6f}, exact={val:.6f}, len={p.size}")
            if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_exact:
                best_exact = val
                best_seq = seq
            if best_exact < 1.2:
                break

    if best_seq is None:
        # Ensure a valid fallback is returned even if exact wrappers misbehave.
        fallback_p = best_p
        if fallback_p is None:
            fallback_p = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        best_seq = scale_for_eval(fallback_p)
        best_exact = try_evaluate(best_seq)
        print(f"Fallback exact={best_exact:.6f}")

    return best_seq
```
